### B7: Live system to replay and export

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T7.1 | E | Replay executes a provider or mutates live state | File-only entry point; subprocess tripwires forbid database, provider, socket and dotenv access. | J; `tests/test_replay_isolation.py` | Verified |
| T7.2 | I | An export leaks secrets or another tenant's history | Current ADMIN authority rechecked before publication; secret-bearing input rejected against the one definition the log guard and the test helper also read (`secrets.py`); tenant-scoped reads. | S; `tests/test_replay_export.py::test_foreign_tenant_bundle_id_collision_cannot_enter_export`, `tests/test_secrets.py` | Verified |
| T7.3 | T | Tampered history, head or bundle content | Chain, retained head, bundle hashes and plan and approval bindings are verified offline. | `tests/test_replay_verify.py` | Verified |
| T7.4 | I | Logs or an event export leak a secret or an untrusted string, or the export skips or reorders history | Logs are schema-only with a closed event catalog and a suppression filter for private text; the export sends the replay export's sanitized representation after its secret check, in sequence order, advancing a per-destination cursor only on acknowledgement, and never writes the chain (`logging_schema.py`, `export.py`, `secrets.py`). | `tests/test_structured_logging.py`, `tests/test_event_export.py` (SE1 to SE13), `tests/test_secrets.py` | Verified |
| T7.5 | T | A one-approver consumption is presented as satisfying a two-approver requirement | Approval captures carry `required_approvers` and every counted approver; a `VERIFIED` capture with fewer distinct approvers than required, a repeated principal or a denial is `INSUFFICIENT_QUORUM` and makes the history incomplete; candidate comparison never assumes recorded approvers satisfy a changed requirement (`replay/verify.py`, `replay/simulate.py`). | `tests/test_approval_quorum.py::test_qa11_replay_captures_every_approver_and_refuses_a_short_quorum` | Verified |
| T7.6 | D | An agent, an Arcade control point or the assistant's model writes text shaped like a secret into an audit event, and the event export refuses that tenant's history at the event for good, and every replay export of the tenant with it | The export's secret check stays as it is, and text from outside is screened before the chain carries it. Admission refuses a request whose `external_case` holds secret-shaped text as `INVALID_REQUEST`, audited by its reason and quoting nothing; the case is the one text of the agent's own that an event carries, written into the first transition before any decision. A contradiction the Arcade extension appends withholds a reported tool identity of that shape (`tool_identity: WITHHELD`) rather than refusing the post hook, which would lose the record that a denied call ran. The assistant refuses a proposed rule holding such text as `INVALID_PROPOSAL`, naming the field. `reference` and the idempotency key reach no audit payload. The screens sit at those boundaries and not in the request model, because stored plans are read back through it (`execution/admission.py`, `arcade/routes.py`, `assistant/proposals.py`, `secrets.py`). Text a person, the owner or an identity provider supplies is T7.7's. | `tests/test_request_secret_screen.py`, `tests/test_event_export.py::test_se9_a_payload_that_trips_the_secret_check_stops_the_export_at_that_event` | Partial, see R34 |
| T7.7 | D | A person, the owner or an identity provider writes text shaped like a secret into an audit event or a policy bundle, and the event export refuses that tenant's history at the event for good, and every replay export of the tenant with it | Text a person types is refused where it is written with the boundary's existing code, never quoting the value, against the one secret definition (`secrets.py`: `secret_shaped`, or `find_secrets` over a manifest entry or a bundle) over exactly what the event or the bundle will carry: reasons (`execution/stops.py`, `execution/modes.py`, `provider/gateway.py` `reconcile_operation`, `execution/reconciliation.py` `reconcile`, `access_control.py`, `approval_rules.py` `change_rule`, `bindings.py` `unbind`, `surfaces/people_pages.py` `deactivate`, `assistant/turns.py` `set_tenant_switch`, `spaces.py`, and a policy activation's reason in `policy/store.py` `activate_bundle`, refused as `INVALID_REASON` at `reason` and reaching the console as 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming `reason`); principal labels, through `administration.screened_label` (`people_pages` `create_approver` and `label_person`, `agents_pages.create_agent`, `admin_pages.issue`, `administration.invite_person`); group, approval rule, space and personal access token names (`administration.create_group`, `access_control._change` with the group's description, `approval_rules.change_rule`, `spaces.create_space` and `rename_space`, `personal_tokens.issue_token`); the owner's signal source labels and producers, context source writers and every context manifest entry (`provisioning.py`), a new executor key id (`administration.register_executor_key`, and `rotate_executor_key`'s new id, never a key a revocation or a compromise report names), a resource's provider id, version and hostname alias and a mapping's operation ref and version (`administration.create_resource`, `create_mapping`), and the hosted sandbox seed, at each of its four writers rather than in the parser every reader of the mounted slots file goes through (in the hosted plugin's own code); an email address's domain, the only part an event carries (`people_pages.set_email`, `administration.invite_person`, `passwords.set_email`, `invitations.create_invitation`); an administrator's note on a monitoring acknowledgement (`monitoring/store.py` `acknowledge` and `acknowledge_many`, refused as `INVALID_NOTE` and reaching the console as 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming `note`); and a policy bundle's whole content in `policy/store.py` `_insert_draft`, the store's one writer of a draft (the harness's `install_bundle`, which the demos and the hosted sandbox seed use, writes fixed content without it), as `INVALID_TEXT` at the text's location, the ruleset first. Where the console takes the text, it answers 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming the field or the location (`surfaces/console_api/`); a command prints the code (`cli.py`, and the hosted plugin's commands), the space commands print `INVALID_NAME` for a reason, the code `spaces._reason` always raised, and the hosted service's own seed command prints a refusal sentence of its own. Text the identity provider supplies is withheld, never refused, by `secrets.withheld`: a subject of that shape in `identity.linked`, `human_sign_in.directory`, `human_sign_in.google` or `human_sign_in.github`, and an issuer in `identity_provider.registered` (`directory_store.py` `register`, `link_self`, `link_person` and `sign_in`; `passwords.bind_identity`; `user_accounts.provider_sign_in`), becomes `WITHHELD` beside `<field>_digest`, while `identity_links`, `principal_identities`, `user_identities` and `identity_providers` keep the real value, so a sign-in finds the same person. The digest is an unkeyed SHA-256 of the exact value: it correlates events across tenants and exports, and a short value can be guessed offline, so it gives correlation, not secrecy. A reader tells a withheld value by its `<field>_digest` key, never by the literal, which a plain value may spell; Arcade's `tool_identity` (T7.6) is withheld with no digest. An Entra display name of that shape governs its agent as `Unnamed agent` (`agents_pages.govern`), and `monitor_agents` keeps the name. `account_links.finish_link`'s subject reaches no audit event: `user_security_change` stores it in `user_identities`, and `account_security.provider_linked` carries the session id and the provider, never the subject (`mfa._event`). The JWT alternative of `SECRET_TEXT` is case-sensitive (`secrets.py`), since every JWT begins `eyJ`, so a lowercase hostname or email domain such as `eyjump.corp.example` is no longer read as one. Every screen sits at a writer, never on a model stored rows are read back through. | `tests/test_text_screen_reasons.py`, `tests/test_policy_store.py::test_an_activation_reason_shaped_like_a_secret_is_refused_unquoted`, `tests/test_console_api_policy.py::test_activation_requires_a_reason_and_records_it`, `tests/test_text_screen_names.py`, `tests/test_text_screen_registry.py`, `tests/test_text_screen_email.py`, `tests/test_text_screen_bundles.py`, `tests/test_text_screen_identity.py`, `tests/test_monitoring_acknowledgement.py::test_mq13_a_note_shaped_like_a_secret_is_refused_and_never_quoted`, `tests/test_text_screen_export.py::test_shaped_text_from_people_and_the_identity_provider_leaves_both_exports_open`, `tests/test_secrets.py::test_a_lowercase_or_uppercase_three_label_name_is_not_a_jwt`, `::test_every_real_jwt_still_matches`; the hosted service's own tests | Partial, see R34 |
| T7.8 | I | A regression fixture leaks a tenant's identifiers, names, free text or secrets, or misstates what replay decided | Every UUID, and every open-text value the kept structure carries, becomes an order-preserving pseudonym: resource and target ids, resource and mapping versions, every fact's source version but a signal fact's (its source's registration number), the version a provider journal reports, an operation ref or a context writer an owner named, a fact's object and a signal's event id, request keys and references, the agent's external case (source, case id and link) on an execution's first transition, stop reasons, every transition reason that is not one of the kernel's own codes, approval comments and rule notes. A label is replaced once, from its own text, at the position it was recorded at and, unless its text is a word the fixture keeps anyway (a catalog name or value, a word of the Cedar language such as `permit` or `when`, one of the kernel's own transition reasons such as `PROVIDER_RESPONSE`, a field name, a value at a field the kernel fills from a closed vocabulary such as a state or a rule id, the published journal key's id, a case link pseudonym's fixed text, or a token of one), wherever else it appears as a whole token, Cedar text included; an operation ref or a version with no letter in it, such as `7`, is replaced only as a whole value or JSON string. So a ref named after the vendor's own operation, or a one-word comment that reads a word the fixture keeps, even a fixture's own field name or a word of its expectations, neither rewrites the history nor stops the build. Times are kept, so a one-word comment that is no kept word but is a whole token of a time, such as `00` or `30`, does stop it (`FIXTURE_NOT_FAITHFUL`), and so, as a survivor, does a label whose text is the pseudonym another label is given. Informational payloads are reduced to the keys replay reads. The build refuses and writes nothing when the source does not verify against its head, a secret is found in the source, the candidates or the output, any source identifier survives anywhere in the output, any collected label that is not a kept word survives anywhere in the sanitized history or the candidates (journal records included; a letterless operation ref or version found only as a whole value or JSON string, and the expectations, replay's own classification of that history held equal to the source's, not searched for labels), a label position holds anything but a pseudonym, or the sanitized history does not verify and replay to the same classified outcomes under the mapping. Reading a fixture applies the same secret screen to the whole fixture, candidates and expectations included, and the same shape check, so neither an export wrapped by hand nor a secret typed into a committed candidate is checked or re-recorded; tenant text typed by hand into a candidate's policy text cannot be told from other policy text without the source, and rests on review of the fixture's diff (R41). A fixture is written readable by its owner alone. Externally executed evidence (an executor's record, an ES256 JWS, a connector record or an asserted settlement) is refused rather than rewritten, and the check compares classified outcomes only (`replay/pseudonyms.py`, `replay/sanitize.py`, `replay/fixture.py`). | RF3, RF5, RF6, RF7, RF8, RF9; `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf5_every_kind_of_label_is_replaced_by_its_typed_pseudonym`, `::test_rf5_the_agents_case_on_the_first_transition_never_reaches_a_fixture`, `::test_rf5_a_kernel_reason_code_is_kept_and_any_other_reason_is_replaced`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_also_a_kept_word_is_replaced_only_where_it_was_recorded`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_a_word_the_fixture_writes_itself_still_becomes_a_fixture`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_a_cedar_word_or_a_kernel_reason_code_stays_where_the_kernel_wrote_it`, `::test_rf5_an_owners_version_or_ref_inside_policy_text_is_replaced_there_too`, `::test_rf5_text_a_person_typed_lends_no_kept_word`, `::test_rf5_a_signal_facts_source_version_is_kept_and_any_other_facts_is_a_label`, `::test_rf5_no_source_identifier_or_label_survives`, `::test_rf5_each_label_is_replaced_once_from_its_own_text`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_reads_as_another_labels_pseudonym_is_refused`, `::test_rf6_externally_executed_evidence_is_refused`, `::test_rf7_a_label_that_survives_inside_other_text_is_refused`, `::test_rf7_a_version_left_inside_policy_text_is_refused`, `::test_rf7_a_one_word_comment_that_is_a_token_of_a_time_is_refused`, `::test_rf3_an_export_wrapped_by_hand_as_a_fixture_is_refused`, `::test_rf3_a_fixture_edited_to_hold_a_secret_is_refused_by_reading`, `tests/test_replay_fixture_cli.py::test_rf8_a_fixture_is_written_readable_by_its_owner_only`, `tests/test_replay_fixture_worlds.py::test_rf9_a_signal_world_fixture_is_deterministic_sanitized_and_checks` | Verified, see R41 |

### B8: Build, dependencies and CI

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T8.1 | T | Vulnerable or substituted dependency | Locked, hash-pinned environments (`uv sync --locked`; the maintained MCP client is hash-locked separately); the `pip-audit` vulnerability audit runs weekly and on demand in the development repository's scheduled security-scans workflow, which retains its finding population for 90 days, and locally on demand; it is not a merge check, and Dependabot security updates are off. | `.github/workflows/security-scans.yml` in the development repository; the public repository runs no scheduled scan | Partial |
| T8.2 | I | A credential committed to the repository | Environment and key files are ignored; `gitleaks` scans the whole history weekly and on demand in the development repository's scheduled security-scans workflow, and with a local binary on demand. It is not a merge check, and a scan that is skipped is recorded as a coverage gap rather than a clean result. | `.github/workflows/security-scans.yml` in the development repository; the public repository runs no scheduled scan | Partial |
| T8.3 | T | Evidence not tied to the tested source | The CI manifest binds artifacts and collected test nodes to the exact commit and lock hashes. | `tests/test_validation_evidence.py::test_manifest_maps_exact_tests_and_excludes_private_files`, `::test_manifest_rejects_results_not_matching_collection` | Verified |
| T8.4 | T | Parsing or canonicalization bugs under unexpected input | Example-based tests, plus Hypothesis property-based and fuzz tests over the request, MCP and monitoring parsers, canonical JSON, the intent, plan and binding hashes and the contract domains. | `tests/test_properties.py` | Verified |
| T8.5 | I | Static analysis limited to open-source tooling | Bandit runs on every change in CI, as a step of the required `verify` job in the development repository and in the `lint` stage of the public repository's, and weekly in the development repository's scheduled scans, which retain its findings. CodeQL and native secret scanning need GitHub Advanced Security on the private development repository and are available on the public one at no cost. No remediation SLA is adopted yet. | `scripts/ci.py` (`stage_lint`); `.github/workflows/security-scans.yml` in the development repository | Accepted |
| T8.6 | T | The runtime image drifts from the locked environment, runs as root or carries private files | Built only from `uv.lock` with base images pinned by digest; non-root uid 10001, whose login shell and empty `~/.ssh` serve only a hosting platform's operator shell (the image runs no SSH server); allowlist build context; CI builds and smoke-tests every image. The development repository's `verify` job then retains a CycloneDX SBOM of the image's own dependency set and a provenance document binding the revision, the locked input digests, both pinned base images and the built image id, with signing and deployment recorded as explicit gaps; the public repository's CI builds, smoke-tests and describes the image the same way, in its `provenance` stage, without retaining the documents, and a release publishes them beside the signed image (T12.6). | The development repository's `verify` job, in its `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which the public repository's own workflow replaces in the export; [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md#release-record-provenance-and-rollback) | Verified |
| T8.7 | T | A vulnerable package in the image's base layer goes unnoticed because only Python dependencies are audited | The scheduled scan builds the runtime image, exports it and scans the tarball with a digest-pinned Trivy container (no Docker socket is exposed), covering operating-system packages and every Python distribution on the filesystem, not only the application environment the SBOM describes; the whole population is retained for 90 days and a HIGH or CRITICAL advisory with a published fix fails the run, with the policy in `scripts/scan_summary.py` rather than a severity flag. It is not a merge check. A finding that cannot be fixed yet is accepted in writing in the development repository's `security/acknowledged-advisories.toml`, beside the scheduled scan that reads it, with a reason and a `review_by` date capped at 180 days, after which the run fails as expired; an unreadable or incomplete record suppresses nothing, and the development repository's test suite validates the committed file rather than leaving it to the weekly scan alone. The public repository carries no such file, and `scripts/scan_summary.py` reads an absent file as acknowledging nothing. | `tests/test_scan_summary.py`, `tests/internal/test_acknowledged_advisories.py::test_the_committed_acknowledgements_are_valid_and_current`; `.github/workflows/security-scans.yml` in the development repository; the public repository runs no scheduled scan | Verified |
| T8.8 | T | A forbidden rendering, transport or TLS flag enters the tree without a reviewer noticing | `tests/test_repository_rules.py` is a table of patterns the tree may never contain, each with the invariant it protects and where that invariant is written down: a connection string rendered with its password, a followed redirect, a transport reading the environment, an echoed SQL parameter, unverified TLS, a bandit marker that does not name its code and its reason, and a hand-written secret assertion; `tests/internal/test_workflow_rules.py` holds the same shape of rule for a traced shell step in a workflow, private-only there because `.github` is. It runs in the suite, so it is a merge check rather than a weekly scan, and bandit has no rule of this shape. A file is allowed only by name and reason in the table, never by an inline comment, and an allowance that no longer matches fails, so an exception disappears the day it is unnecessary. | `tests/test_repository_rules.py`, `tests/internal/test_workflow_rules.py` | Verified |
| T8.9 | I | The self-hosted console build carries hosted code (a hosted page, a call to a hosted route, hosted copy), or the hosted gateway serves the core console | The core build lists every module it loaded, and every asset it emitted, and fails on one under `console/src/hosted/` (`console/scripts/editions.ts`); ESLint refuses an import of a hosted module, static or lazy, in core's files under `console/src/` and `console/scripts/`, and `@edition` and `@route-tree` there outside `console/src/main.tsx`; the core build's own tests read the packaged build's document for its edition, `core`, its manifest for no module under `src/hosted/`, and its scripts and document for the hosted service's paths as strings, which the refusal of modules cannot see: `/signup`, `/invitations` and every path of the hosted plugin's committed console API document, each matched only where it ends, with a parameter standing for any one segment; the hosted deployment sets the build argument `CONSOLE_EDITION=hosted` on its gateway alone; CI reads `core` from the image it builds, and in the development repository its `verify` job also builds the image's console stage with that argument and reads `hosted` from it, a check the public repository's CI does not make because its tree carries no hosted edition; and `/health/ready` reports `console`, the edition the served build names. Core's pages take the hosted service's addresses from the deployment's service links (T12.5), so the core build carries no hosted address. One gap remains. A hosted gateway built without the argument serves the core console: readiness reports that and refuses nothing, so only the release check a person makes by hand after each hosted gateway deploy finds it. | CE1, CE2, CE7, CE10; hosted: CE8; `tests/test_console_serving.py::test_the_packaged_build_is_the_core_edition` and `tests/internal/test_blueprint.py::test_the_gateway_builds_the_hosted_console_and_no_other_service_names_an_edition`, `tests/test_service_links.py::test_without_service_links_no_page_links_to_a_service_page` | Partial |
| T8.10 | R | Documentation claims a compatibility the repository cannot prove: an integration listed as tested whose test was renamed or never runs, or a platform named as validated with no record of the trial | `docs/INTEGRATIONS.md` is rendered from `integrations/manifest.json`, and the manifest test refuses a row whose claim outruns its evidence. A `fixture-tested` row names a mode other than Unknown, the ExecBound version it pins and at least one test, each a function its file defines, and every test any row names must be a node this suite collects, in one collection over all of them, so a test renamed or moved in one pull request fails it while the manifest still cites the old place. A `platform-validated` row names a trial record, and every record a row names must exist with its host, version, date, transport, gateway commit and fixture seed and its scenarios. A `research-only` row claims no mode, no tested version, no test and no trial. A recipe a row names must exist, no row and no line of the page uses a commercial word, and the page's own links resolve. Whether the page is still the manifest's current rendering is checked only in the development repository, because that check also compares the site's copy of the register, which a self-hosted export does not carry. What this holds is each row's claim against its own evidence at the versions it names, and nothing about a platform beyond the recorded runs; it judges a row's mode only as far as holding a research-only row to Unknown and a fixture-tested one away from it, so a path that can be skipped says so in its own limits (R35). | `tests/test_integrations_manifest.py::test_each_row_claims_only_what_its_evidence_supports`, `::test_every_test_a_row_names_is_one_this_suite_collects`, `::test_a_trial_a_row_names_is_a_record_with_the_interview_fields`, `::test_the_integrations_page_has_no_commercial_words_and_its_links_resolve` | Verified |

### B9: Gate caller to gateway

The [external execution checkpoint](CHECKPOINT.md) puts the last mile on the customer's credential, so this boundary has more Accepted rows than any other. Each says which of the guide's two tables it comes from.

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T9.1 | E | A caller selects Gate for itself, names an executor, declares a precondition class or claims a tier | All four are owner-provisioned bundle content or owner-registered deployment configuration; `external_executor`, `precondition_class` and `expected_version` are refused on the wire as unknown fields, the class is keyed by the provider binding the frozen plan names, and the tier is derived from what ExecBound could check rather than from anything the report claims (`policy/models.py`, `execution/admission.py`, `execution/outcomes.py`). | GC15; `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Verified |
| T9.2 | E | A grant is used as authority to execute, approve, dispatch or reconcile through ExecBound | Admission returns no dispatch ticket for a Gate mapping and the runtime's checkpoint branch never reaches the connector table; `take_dispatch` refuses a row carrying a report window as well as one already stamped; the grant's only power is to settle the one execution it names. The MCP tool description states that a grant is neither an execution nor an approval. | GC1, GC18; `tests/test_checkpoint.py`, `tests/test_checkpoint_workflow.py`, `tests/test_mcp_surface.py` | Verified |
| T9.3 | S | A forged, replayed or cross-execution report settles a grant | One report secret per execution, returned once and retained only as a SHA-256 digest in `owner_hash`, compared in constant time; another principal's execution answers `NOT_FOUND`; a settled row accepts only a restatement of its own tier, outcome and reason, and every other report against one is refused rather than discarded (`execution/outcomes.py`). | GC6, GC9, GC11; `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Verified |
| T9.4 | S | A forged attestation, a registered key nobody holds the private half of, or one everybody holds the private half of | Public keys only, Ed25519 or ES256, so no secret at rest can forge the attested tier; the ten small-order points and every non-canonical encoding are refused at registration and again when the ring is built, because such a key makes one fixed signature verify every message; the executor protocol's published test keys, whose private halves its vectors publish, are refused at registration and rotation in every spelling, attest nothing if a ring already holds one, and count as no live key to admission, bundle validation or the revocation guard, and its published journal keys, and the one replay's regression fixtures are re-signed under, are refused as any connector's, Gate account's or sample key in every form HMAC-SHA256 reads as the same key (`provider/published_keys.py`); the signed envelope carries its own domain and version and binds the key id, so it can never be relabelled or be the same bytes as a provider journal's (`provider/executor.py`, `administration.py`, `runtime.py`). | `tests/test_checkpoint_evidence.py`, `tests/test_admin_cli.py`, `tests/test_published_test_keys.py` | Verified |
| T9.5 | E | A stolen executor key settles grants it was holding, refunding reservations and freeing targets | A retirement is deliberately backward-permissive, so a separate act records a compromise, which the verifier reads retroactively and which reaches every id of the tenant carrying that public key; it is asked inside the settlement's own fenced transaction, so a cut-off committing while a record is being verified still refuses; recording it may empty the ring, and admission then refuses new grants for that tenant (`administration.py`, `provider/executor.py`, migration `0026_key_compromise`). | `tests/test_checkpoint_evidence.py`, `tests/test_checkpoint.py`, `tests/test_admin_cli.py` | Verified, see R13 |
| T9.6 | T | An executor frees its own target or refunds its own budget by what it says | A reservation and a canonical claim are committed before the grant is issued; the claim is released exactly when a verifier returned the evidence, so **no assertion** releases a claim or refunds a reservation. What an assertion may settle is narrower than a reader of the earlier wording would take: an asserted `EXECUTED` settles and confirms its impact, an asserted `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` with reason `REPORTED_FAILURE` settles **terminally** and confirms its impact too — so a stated failure costs exactly what a stated success costs, and there is nothing to gain by choosing between them — while an asserted `FAILED_NOT_EXECUTED`, the one outcome that claims the vendor was never reached, and an unknown result settle nothing at all, which is the refund on absent evidence the contract forbids (`execution/outcomes.py`). One consequence belongs in this row rather than in the key-compromise row above: **revocation as `COMPROMISED` no longer freezes such a grant.** The verifier is loaded only for a report presenting a signed record, so the executor's or the control point's bare word still settles the row, confirms its impact and puts it in front of the recovery run, which frees the target at the act window; what the revocation takes away for good is the tenant's ability to *answer* that word, because the correction needs a key usable at the grant instant. | GC7, GC8, AL12, AL13, AL18, AL33; `tests/test_checkpoint.py::test_no_route_to_an_asserted_execution_releases_the_claim`, `tests/test_arcade.py` | Verified |
| T9.7 | T | A report settles an operation ExecBound dispatched, or a provider journal settles a grant | The report route re-reads the owner-provisioned bundle the execution was frozen under, inside the fence, and refuses any execution whose mapping does not declare an external executor, before the secret is compared; the connector paths refuse a row carrying a report window; and each side refuses the other's kind of verifier, so no settlement rests on the wrong kind of evidence for the row it names (`execution/outcomes.py`, `execution/reconciliation.py`, `provider/gateway.py`). | GC9, GC10; `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Verified |
| T9.8 | D | An executor takes grants and never reports, or spams refused reports | Outstanding grants are capped at eight per principal inside the fence and a refused attempt rolls back entirely; the report route shares the runtime's capacity bound, so refusals cannot serialize a tenant's writers; an unreported grant is swept into retained uncertainty and shown in the uncertain queue. The budget it holds until then is the point: a grant charges its scopes exactly as a dispatch does. | GC16; `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Verified, see R14 |
| T9.9 | R | An executor acts and never asks, ignores a denial, or acts twice on one grant | Nothing prevents any of the three, and the guide says so in the same table. ExecBound has no record of the first at all; the second leaves a denial in the history with nothing charged; the third is invisible and is charged once. Only monitoring coverage of that path can notice an action with no matching checkpoint, and it is classified as missing evidence, never as a proven bypass. | [CHECKPOINT.md](CHECKPOINT.md) §5 | Accepted, see R7 |
| T9.10 | T | The vendor's target changes between the grant and the executor's action | The frozen expected version travels with the grant and is only as strong as the vendor's conditional write. Under `ASSERTED_BY_EXECUTOR` the executor can only compare and refuse, and under `UNAVAILABLE` a concurrent change is undetectable; what records that the question was unanswerable is the class the grant carried, which its `execution.checkpoint_granted` events retain and the execution records at the first answer. Pair an `UNAVAILABLE` binding with an approval obligation. | GC12; `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Accepted |
| T9.11 | S | An operator declares a precondition class the vendor does not offer | Nothing checks it, and nothing can: the class is a statement about a vendor's API. A declaration naming a binding the running catalog does not bind is refused at load, and an undeclared binding answers `UNAVAILABLE`, so the failure mode is an operator's own overclaim rather than a silent default. | [SURFACES.md](SURFACES.md#private-configuration-and-startup) | Accepted, see R15 |
| T9.12 | E | A compromised Gate caller's credential also dispatches through ExecBound | A Gate caller holds a vendor credential and is a different trust class from a protected agent, which holds none, so a principal should serve Gate mappings or Execute mappings and not both. **Nothing in the code enforces that separation today**; admission refuses a Gate mapping whose account already has a connector, which keeps one operation from being both, and says nothing about a principal that has one mapping of each kind. | [CHECKPOINT.md](CHECKPOINT.md) | Open, see R16 |
| T9.13 | R | An administrator settles a grant its executor is still authorized to act on | Deliberately admitted: the evidence decides and not the actor, so an executor-signed record presented by an administrator is the record the outcome route would accept. It closes ExecBound's route on that executor and does not stop it acting at the vendor, and the console says so where the administrator is about to act. | [RECOVERY.md](RECOVERY.md#uncertain-checkpoint-grants); `tests/test_checkpoint.py` | Accepted |
| T9.14 | S | A forged or replayed ES256 JWS settles a grant | The header is parsed strictly (`alg: "ES256"` and `kid` only) and the payload strictly (the domain, version 1 and the seven record fields, nothing else); the 64-byte concatenated r-and-s signature is verified over the exact signing input; the binding checks the same four fields and `reported_at` an Ed25519 record checks, against the same command. A second, differently signed JWS can only restate a settled outcome, which retains and appends nothing. | GC6, TG7, TG8; `tests/test_gate_evidence.py` | Verified |
| T9.15 | E | Algorithm confusion: a token labelled for one scheme verifies under a key of another | The algorithm always comes from the registered key's own `algorithm` column, never from the header or from anything the message claims: an Ed25519 key never verifies a JWS and an ES256 key never verifies the Ed25519 envelope, so a header naming `HS256` keyed with the public key, `RS256`, `EdDSA` or a mismatched `kid` is refused before a signature is checked. | TG8; `tests/test_gate_evidence.py` | Verified |
| T9.16 | S | A provider's journal for one command, execution or operation settles a grant it does not name | `CheckpointVerifier` compares `journal.record.command` against the frozen provider command for this execution byte for byte, as a command hash and then as canonical JSON (the operation, account, intent hash, plan hash, target, version and arguments together), and refuses a journal dated before the grant. A `checkpoint_providers` entry is keyed by tenant, account and key id, so a journal signed for another tenant's or another account's Gate mapping verifies under no key this one holds. The outcome route also refuses any journal, before checking it, for a grant whose execution records the class `UNAVAILABLE`, which carries no `provider_command` for a journal to be of, even one of the command frozen with the plan; the class is the one recorded at the first answer, and a grant issued before it was recorded is read as before (`execution/outcomes.py`, `execution/admission.py`). | TG3, TG14; `tests/test_gate_evidence.py`, `tests/test_gate_vendor_walk.py`, `tests/test_checkpoint_journal_class.py` | Verified |
| T9.17 | I | A reader of the gateway's private configuration forges a provider journal | The journal key is HMAC and sits in the same file as connector evidence keys, so a reader of that configuration is in the same trust boundary a connector's own journal key is: the key is verification-only, the gateway never calls the provider with it, and the executor never holds it, but the file's own confidentiality is what stands behind it rather than a cryptographic separation ExecBound enforces. | [CHECKPOINT.md](CHECKPOINT.md); [SURFACES.md](SURFACES.md#private-configuration-and-startup) | Accepted, see R3 |
| T9.18 | E | The Tines ES256 signing credential's reach is wider than the story that uses it | Tines refuses a credential in a cryptographic function while domain restriction is enabled, so the signing credential carries no host restriction and any story in the team with access to it can produce a valid attestation; the report secret is readable in the same team's event data for the life of the grant. Neither is a defect the protocol can close from ExecBound's side: the recipe states both and recommends a dedicated Tines team and short event retention. | [CHECKPOINT.md](CHECKPOINT.md#limitations) | Accepted, see R27 |

### B10: Control point to hook surface

The [Arcade logic extension](ARCADE.md) makes ExecBound the decision service behind a platform's hooks, so this boundary is about a caller that calls on **every** tool invocation and about the correlation table that stands behind it. Most rows here are fixture-verified; one live trial on 18 September 2026 observed blocking and state end to end ([ARCADE.md](ARCADE.md)).

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T10.1 | E | A hook field selects the mapping, the action, the target, the incident or the tier | The tool map is deployment configuration in the file that already carries connectors and precondition classes, and it is part of the configuration shape a key reload may not change; the exact toolkit, name and version pick one declaration and nothing else does; the target and the incident are each one **declared** input key taken by name with no expression language, path syntax or fallback; the tier is derived from what ExecBound could check. Reported metadata may only **refuse** (`arcade/map.py`). | AL8, AL9, AL10, AL24, AL34 | Verified |
| T10.2 | T | An input the map does not model changes what the vendor does behind a decision made without it | `inputs` is an allowlist and any other key is refused before a kernel call, so the residual is a brittle tool rather than a smuggled field: a vendor adding an optional input breaks every governed call of that tool until an operator declares it. | AL9 | Verified, see R18 |
| T10.3 | E | The extension permits something the kernel did not | It is a translator: it builds the wire bodies `ActionRequest` and `OutcomeReport` already define and calls `Runtime.execute` and `Runtime.report`, opens no transaction of its own around a decision and has no other path into the kernel; everything it decides by itself can only deny, and a tenant emergency stop is refused ahead of both of its `OK` answers (`arcade/routes.py`). | AL1, AL5, AL6, AL22 | Verified |
| T10.4 | S | A duplicate or racing delivery gets a second `OK`, or a lost one strands a grant the platform is told to act on | The `OK` rule is keyed on what the kernel returned and not on what the extension recorded: a `DISPATCH_COMMITTED` answer is `OK` only while this Arcade execution's own correlation row holds that grant's report secret. The kernel mints a grant's secret exactly once and a re-grant carries none, so the concurrent second Arcade id, the abandoned-deadline row and the crashed row are all refused with no special case. | AL2, AL3, AL4 | Verified |
| T10.5 | I | A reader of the correlation table settles a tenant's row or frees its target | Deliberately admitted and stated as the bound rather than claimed against: the secret is usable only inside the report window, it is nulled at settlement and cleared opportunistically afterwards, and a reader that presents it closes the row and lets the scheduled run free the target once the act window has passed. What no reader of it can do is reduce what the tenant is charged, because both statements the carrier can make confirm the impact. | AL18 | Accepted, see R17 |
| T10.6 | R | The platform reports a failure the tenant cannot answer | A reported failure settles terminally at `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, tier `ASSERTED`, with the impact confirmed — which is what keeps the project working, because silence wedges the intent. The tenant's answer is the correction: an ADMIN-signed non-execution through `reconcile`, on one row, restoring exactly the power the same administrator held over that execution one state earlier. It is refused in every other direction, needs a key usable at the grant instant, and an ADMIN performs it from the Activity page's settlement control on that row. | AL13, AL20, AL30, AL31, AL33 | Accepted, see R19, R20 |
| T10.7 | D | An ungoverned toolkit's traffic drives the tenant's audit head | An ungoverned pass makes no kernel call, no execution row, no reservation and **no audit event**, asserted against the tenant's chain head before and after. The reason is the lock rather than the volume: `append_event` takes the per-tenant serializing structure, and the ungoverned pass is the success path of every tool call in the project. The residual is that neither the chain nor the correlation table can say how many such calls passed. | AL8 | Accepted, see R18 |
| T10.8 | D | The deployment under load stops answering in a shape the platform can act on | The boundary rewrites the application's own non-hook answers per route: on `POST /pre` a 429 becomes `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and everything else that is not a 200 or a 401 becomes `CHECK_FAILED`, so an overloaded pre hook blocks whatever failure mode the project is configured with; on `POST /post` nothing is rewritten, because converting an error to `OK` would tell the platform its report was accepted when nothing was settled. The extension's own deadline is required with no default and answers `CHECK_FAILED` rather than the retryable code. | AL7, AL21, AL25, AL32 | Verified |
| T10.9 | I | Third-party identity material or a vendor string reaches ExecBound's storage, logs or the agent's context | `authorization[].oauth2.at` is dropped at the edge — not parsed, not retained, not logged — and `context.secrets` carries names and no values; `output` is opaque, dropped before anything else happens and never retained or logged; `error_message` carries a stable ExecBound code, the execution id where one exists and, for a pending approval, the approvals page, and never an input value, a vendor string, a rule id, a secret or a continuation. | AL5, AL14 | Verified |
| T10.10 | E | A second logic extension changes the inputs after this one decided, or a denied call runs anyway | Neither can be prevented and the guide says so in the same table. Two arrivals are **recorded**: a post hook whose correlation row is a denial, and one whose `inputs` differ from the frozen ones, each appended once to the tenant's chain under `execution.control_point_contradiction` exactly when it moves the correlation row. Both are missing or contradictory evidence and neither is a proven bypass. | AL16, AL17 | Accepted |
| T10.11 | S | An operator declares a precondition class for a binding this carrier serves | Nothing checks it, and on this path it can mean nothing at all: the hook response has no field for a grant and the extension sets no override, so no version reaches Arcade and no record can say whether the target moved. A class declared for such a binding is an overclaim on every page and report that shows it; the honest declaration is none. | [ARCADE.md](ARCADE.md) | Accepted, see R15 |
| T10.12 | S | A caller reaches policy through a fact the surface does not evaluate | The one context field the extension fills is the incident id, and it is a **claim**: `registry.incident` binds it to the resolved target under the mapping's own tenant, account, provider and resource type inside an RLS-scoped transaction, so an id naming nothing, another tenant's, another account's and another resource's are each refused. What it does not bind is *which* of the target's incidents, so a second or a closed one resolves and the tenant's **policy pack** is what refuses it on the frozen facts. `severity`, `environment`, `role` and `criticality` are never set from a hook. | AL9, AL34 | Verified |

### B11: Console to model provider

The console assistant puts a model between a person and the tenant's records. The boundary is about what reaches the model, what comes back from it, and who can read either. The engine is `execbound/assistant/` and its console routes are `surfaces/console_api/assistant.py`.

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T11.1 | E | The assistant reads or changes something the asking person could not | Every tool is a thin wrapper over a function a page already uses, run with the person's own session inside the tenant fence and rechecked per call; there is no generic query, no tool that writes and no tool that reaches a provider; a page function's refusal is a `found: false` answer; an agent principal can own no turn (`assistant/tools.py`, `assistant/turns.py`). | AS3, AS4; `tests/test_assistant_tools.py::test_an_approver_reads_a_scoped_request_whole_and_nothing_outside_the_scope`, `tests/test_assistant_tools.py::test_agent_reads_are_the_administrators_and_every_string_is_enveloped`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_turns_are_private_to_the_person_who_asked` | Verified |
| T11.2 | T | Text an agent, a provider or a tenant user wrote instructs the model, or reaches the console as markup | Every string from tenant data travels inside an `untrusted` envelope the instructions declare data; the answer is structured output of plain paragraphs the server stores as text; an id becomes a citation only if a tool returned it in the same turn; the console renders text under its policy (`assistant/tools.py`, `assistant/render.py`, `assistant/loop.py`). | AS5; `tests/test_assistant_loop.py::test_injected_instructions_in_tenant_data_reach_the_model_only_as_data`, `tests/test_assistant_client.py::test_an_answer_keeps_only_the_ids_this_turn_saw_and_no_control_characters` | Verified |
| T11.3 | I | A credential, token, code or key reaches the model | Tool projections select no secret column; every result passes `find_secrets` before it is sent and a hit fails the turn with `REFUSED_SECRET`, audited, never retried; the transport records what it was sent so the test asserts the absence (`assistant/tools.py`, `assistant/loop.py`). | AS6; `tests/test_assistant_loop.py::test_a_secret_in_a_tool_result_fails_the_turn_and_never_reaches_the_transport`, `tests/test_assistant_tools.py::test_a_secret_shaped_string_in_tenant_data_ends_the_turn_before_it_leaves` | Verified |
| T11.4 | E | An explanation tells an approver what to decide | The answer schema has no field for a recommendation, the instructions forbid one, and the renderer refuses a fixed phrase list in an approval context, storing the turn as `FAILED`/`RECOMMENDATION` with no answer (`assistant/render.py`, `assistant/loop.py`). | AS7; `tests/test_assistant_loop.py::test_an_explanation_of_an_approval_cannot_carry_a_recommendation`, `tests/test_assistant_client.py::test_a_recommendation_in_an_approval_context_fails_the_answer_and_nowhere_else` | Verified |
| T11.5 | I | One person reads another's questions or answers, or the audit chain carries either | Turn rows are read by the asker alone and written only through definer functions that check the actor owns the turn; the audit events carry the turn id, tools, counts and reasons and never text; retirement nulls the text at thirty days (`migrations/versions/0038_assistant_turns.py`, `assistant/turns.py`). | AS2, AS8; `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_turns_are_private_to_the_person_who_asked`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_retirement_nulls_the_text_at_thirty_days_and_keeps_the_counts`, `tests/test_assistant_loop.py::test_a_turn_reads_through_a_tool_answers_with_citations_and_is_audited_without_text` | Verified |
| T11.6 | D | A turn runs unbounded, or a tenant's use runs up the provider bill | Eight tool calls, sixty seconds and two thousand output tokens each end a turn with its reason and nothing is retried past a bound; a turn left running reads abandoned at ninety seconds; the operator's per-person hourly and per-tenant daily caps are counted in the transaction that creates a turn (`assistant/loop.py`, `assistant/turns.py`). | AS9; `tests/test_assistant_loop.py::test_the_bounds_each_end_the_turn_with_their_reason_and_nothing_is_retried_past_them`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_the_caps_refuse_the_next_turn_and_are_counted_in_the_same_transaction`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_a_turn_left_running_reads_abandoned_after_ninety_seconds` | Verified |
| T11.7 | I | The provider's key is written to the database, a log or a diagnostic, or the feature exists where nobody chose it | The key arrives only as `EXECBOUND_ASSISTANT_API_KEY`, is merged into the block by `boot.py` under the mail password's rule (one place, never both, a refusal names the variable and never the value), is consumed from the environment, and lives on a frozen value whose repr hides it; without the block the feature does not exist, and with it a tenant's switch is off until an ADMIN sets it with a reason, audited (`boot.py`, `surfaces/configuration.py`, `assistant/config.py`, `assistant/turns.py`). | AS1; `tests/test_boot.py::test_the_assistant_key_is_merged_into_its_block_and_scrubbed`, `tests/test_surface_configuration.py::test_the_assistant_block_is_optional_and_makes_the_feature_exist`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py::test_the_switch_is_off_until_an_admin_turns_it_on_with_a_reason` | Verified |
| T11.8 | E | An assistant address is reachable where no model was configured, or for a tenant that never turned it on | The area module registers no route unless the configuration carries an `assistant` block, so the addresses do not exist rather than refusing; with the block, a turn for a tenant whose switch is off is refused `ASSISTANT_OFF`, and the switch is an ADMIN's with a reason, audited (`surfaces/console_api/assistant.py`, `assistant/turns.py`). | AS10, AS12; `tests/test_console_api_assistant.py::test_the_addresses_exist_only_where_a_model_is_configured`, `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_an_assistant_block_adds_its_routes_and_changes_nothing_else` | Verified |
| T11.9 | I | One person reads another's turn through the routes, or an agent credential reaches one | Every route reads the acting person's own rows and answers 404 for another's, on the read, the stream and the opened mark alike; an agent credential is refused 403 and audited on every assistant address, and the route inventory pins all seven as session routes (`surfaces/console_api/assistant.py`). | AS11; `tests/test_console_api_assistant.py::test_a_turn_is_the_askers_and_no_one_elses`, `tests/test_console_api_assistant.py::test_every_route_makes_the_refusals_the_console_api_owes`, `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_an_assistant_route_refuses_an_agent_credential` | Verified |
| T11.10 | D | A stream outlives its turn, or a person watching one holds a connection open indefinitely | The stream polls the turn row on an interval and closes on the turn's ending or at its own deadline, by which time a reader has marked an unfinished turn abandoned; it holds no channel to the loop, so a restart or a second process changes nothing it reports (`assistant/events.py`). | AS13; `tests/test_assistant_events.py::test_the_stream_closes_at_its_deadline_saying_what_the_row_says`, `tests/test_console_api_assistant.py::test_a_turn_answers_into_its_row_and_the_stream_ends_with_the_rows_own_ending` | Verified |
| T11.11 | R | A packaged help text tells a person something the product does not do | Each text is held to the document that states its facts by phrases that must appear in both, as the integrations manifest holds its claims; the texts carry no advice about a decision and read nothing of the tenant's (`assistant/help/`). | AS14; `tests/test_assistant_help.py::test_each_text_says_what_its_document_says` | Verified |
| T11.12 | E | A drafted rule reaches policy without a person, or widens what a bundle governs | The proposal path writes nothing: it creates no draft, adds no rule and activates nothing, and its answer is a form an ADMIN submits through the rules page, after which validation and activation recompile as always. A draft's mappings are the draft's, so a proposal can only name actions that draft already serves (`assistant/proposals.py`). | AS16, AS18; `tests/test_assistant_rules.py::test_a_proposal_is_a_rule_the_compiler_took_read_back_in_the_editors_own_words`, `tests/test_assistant_rules.py::test_only_a_draft_written_as_rules_can_be_drafted_into` | Verified |
| T11.13 | T | A model's answer reaches the form as something other than a valid rule | The answer is constrained to the rule model's own JSON schema, validated with `Rule.model_validate_json` (the validator the form uses) and compiled with the draft before it is offered; an unknown fact, an out-of-domain value, an unmapped action, a duplicate id or an answer that is not a rule at all is refused with the field that caused it (`assistant/proposals.py`, `policy/rulesets.py`). | AS17; `tests/test_assistant_rules.py::test_a_proposal_the_compiler_would_refuse_is_refused_here_with_its_field`, `tests/test_assistant_rules.py::test_an_answer_that_is_not_a_rule_at_all_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written` | Verified |
| T11.14 | I | A proposal is drafted for a draft the person cannot read, or by someone who cannot edit rules | The draft is read with the asking person's own authority through the store, so a bundle they cannot read refuses as the page refuses it, and the route is an administrator's alone (`assistant/proposals.py`, `surfaces/console_api/assistant.py`). | AS18; `tests/test_assistant_rules.py::test_the_route_offers_the_form_to_an_administrator_and_refuses_everyone_else` | Verified |

### B12: Development repository to public export

The self-hosted source is published by copying it, one commit at a time, from the development repository into a public one. Files cross one way, written by the exporter; a contribution crosses back only when a maintainer applies it. Nothing here runs in a deployment: each row is about what the published source carries, and who can change it.

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T12.1 | I | A private file, a secret, a private name or a link back into the development repository leaves in an export | The export manifest names what may leave: code by pattern, less the hosted plugin, its tests, its console edition and the tests of unpublished files; scripts and root files one by one; and the documentation site's published set. The build reads one commit, never the working tree, and checks the manifest in both directions, so a written file it does not name and an entry that names nothing are both refused. A link into the development repository loses its link and keeps its text, and one that survives is refused. A scrub then reads every file of the built tree with the documentation site's rules, the secret definition, the private names, and the export's own rules for a hosted database's project reference, a tenant's own domain, an address outside the reserved example domains, the hosted service's address and, in documents, the hosted plugin's name. A finding is refused unless an allowance names its file and a reason, an allowance that accepts nothing is refused too, and a file the scrub cannot read is refused rather than skipped. Publishing refuses to run without the private names, and the first export is reviewed file by file before the public one is made (the development repository's exporter, `scripts/export_foss.py`). | hosted: EX1, EX2, EX3; `tests/internal/test_export_foss.py::test_the_build_copies_what_the_manifest_names_and_nothing_else`, `::test_this_repository_manifest_names_only_what_is_here`, `::test_the_build_refuses_a_link_it_cannot_take_out`, `::test_the_scrub_refuses_what_it_is_for`, `::test_a_private_name_is_refused_in_any_file`, `::test_a_stale_allowance_is_refused`, `tests/internal/test_export_publish.py::test_publishing_refuses_without_private_names` | Partial, see R29 |
| T12.2 | E | A fork's pull request runs code on the maintainers' own machines, or it, or anyone with write access to the development repository, reaches the credential that writes the public repository | The public repository's CI runs on GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` runners alone, with every action pinned to a full commit and a token that reads the repository and nothing else; the development repository's self-hosted runners are registered to it alone. The job that publishes an export runs on `ubuntu-latest` too, so the key that writes the public repository never shares a machine with any other job. Only the people that job's workflow names can run it: its first step, before any step reads a secret, refuses unless both the person who dispatched the run and whoever started its latest attempt are named, and the environment that holds the key deploys from `main` alone, so the list changes only through a reviewed change there. | EX5; hosted: EX7; `tests/internal/test_public_workflow.py::test_the_workflow_runs_on_github_hosted_ubuntu_alone`, `::test_every_action_is_pinned_to_a_full_commit`, `::test_the_token_reads_the_repository_and_nothing_else`, `::test_the_publishing_job_runs_on_github_hosted_ubuntu_alone`, `tests/internal/test_export_publish.py::test_only_the_people_the_workflow_names_can_run_it`, `::test_the_named_people_check_runs_before_any_secret_is_read`; the publishing job's runner is set in the development repository's `.github/workflows/export-publish.yml` | Verified |
| T12.3 | I | A security fix is published before the hosted service runs it | A merged pull request labelled `embargo` blocks an export while its merge commit or its head is in the source commit's history and not in the history of the commit the hosted service's readiness reports; the lower pull requests of a stack count by their heads. Readiness that cannot be read or reports no commit, and a labelled pull request whose commits the clone does not hold, are refused rather than passed. Without the label an export to the private test repository passes and says so, and the public repository is refused, so deleting or renaming the label cannot switch the rule off. The label is a maintainer's to apply: a fix nobody labels is not held (the development repository's exporter, `scripts/export_foss.py`). | hosted: EX6; `tests/internal/test_export_publish.py::test_an_embargoed_merge_hosted_does_not_run_yet_is_refused`, `::test_an_embargoed_merge_hosted_already_runs_passes`, `::test_a_stacked_embargoed_pull_request_is_found_by_its_head`, `::test_an_embargoed_merge_is_refused_when_readiness_cannot_show_hosted_runs_it`, `::test_the_public_target_is_refused_without_the_embargo_label` | Verified |
| T12.4 | T | Code contributed to the public repository reaches the development repository or the hosted service without review | Nothing flows back on its own. The public repository's `main` changes only through export pull requests, and a public contribution is applied in the development repository by a maintainer, after review and a check for the contributor agreement, as an ordinary pull request that passes that repository's own checks, with its author kept and a trailer naming the public pull request. The script that applies it refuses any head but the one reviewed, a merge commit and a commit its author did not sign off, and puts the result on a branch of its own, from the export the pull request was written against. Each export run is started by one of the people the publishing workflow names. This is review by people, not a control the code enforces. | hosted: EX11; `tests/internal/test_apply_public_pr.py::test_a_pull_request_that_is_not_the_one_reviewed_is_refused`, `::test_a_merge_commit_is_refused`, `::test_a_commit_its_author_did_not_sign_off_is_refused`; the public repository's `CONTRIBUTING.md` | Accepted |

Without either, none of those links renders but Source, which a `service` block may point at another copy or turn off with `null`, and a value that is not an `https` link with a host or a plain address is refused, so nothing in one can add a header to a `mailto:` link (`service_links.py`, `plugins.py`). | T12.6 | S | A release publishes an image nobody approved, from a commit CI did not pass or under a version the code does not carry, or an image a user cannot tell from a forgery | The public repository's release workflow runs on a `v*` tag alone, in two jobs on GitHub-hosted runners. `build` holds a token that can only read, so the third-party code it runs (the image build, the locked sync and the SBOM generator) can push, sign and publish nothing. Before it builds anything, `scripts/release_preflight.py` refuses a tag that is not `v<major>.<minor>.<patch>` or does not point at the commit, a package version in any project file or the lock that is not the tag's, missing notes, a commit off `main`, an image name off `ghcr.io` or carrying a tag or a digest, and a commit on which `ci.yml` has not passed for a push to `main`. `publish` waits for a maintainer's approval in the `release` environment, and refuses before it pushes unless the run carries one. The release record names the approval, the CI run and each published file's digest, and provenance is amended only for a build whose SBOM matched its image. Four gaps remain. Until a version tag is pushed, the workflow is held by tests rather than by a run. ### B13: Security signal producer to gateway

A producer is an out-of-process detector, first an EDR. It holds one credential that opens one route, and it can never read, decide, approve or dispatch. What its verdict means is the operator's mapping in the policy bundle, applied by one function, `signals/mapping.combine`, which resolution, evaluation and replay all call. Each row names the tests that hold it.

| ID | STRIDE | Threat | Mitigation | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T13.1 | S | A forged or stolen producer token | Each source has its own expiring `sg1.` token, stored only as a SHA-256 digest the runtime role cannot read; the owner command writes the token once to a private file and audits without it; rotation keeps the previous token only through its grace window, and revocation is immediate, final and ends a grace window too; `signal_admit` authenticates inside the fence, a rotation committed between its two reads refuses the old token, and every credential refusal reads alike to the caller; the tenant, the source and its scope come from the registration, never the envelope (`signals/tokens.py`, `provisioning.py`, `signal_admit` in `migrations/versions/0068_security_signals.py`). What a thief holding a live token can do before it is revoked is T13.18. | SG2; `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_an_unauthenticated_call_stores_and_records_nothing`, `::test_the_previous_token_works_only_inside_its_grace`, `::test_a_rotation_committed_between_the_two_reads_refuses_the_old_token`, `tests/test_signal_registration.py::test_registration_writes_the_token_once_privately_and_audits_without_it`, `::test_rotation_keeps_the_old_token_only_through_its_grace`, `::test_revocation_is_immediate_and_final`, `::test_revocation_inside_a_grace_window_ends_the_previous_token_too`, `tests/test_signal_schema.py::test_the_runtime_never_reads_a_producer_digest`, `::test_check_runtime_refuses_a_runtime_that_can_read_a_producer_digest`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_every_credential_refusal_answers_the_same_and_records_its_reason` | Verified, see R30 |
| T13.2 | S | The protected agent attests its own target's verdict: `compromised` to take the review out of an isolation, or `clean` to pass a check | A producer token is its own credential kind: the intake refuses an agent credential and every other route refuses a producer token, and registering a source is an owner command whose actor must be an ADMIN. `clean` needs liveness wherever a mapping is applied: validation and activation refuse a mapping to `clean` over a source not registered as attesting liveness, and `signals/mapping.combine` refuses a combined `clean` when any record it combines has `attests_liveness` false, a field every frozen `SignalRecord` must carry, so evaluation and replay refuse it over frozen records under whatever bundle they apply (`signals/tokens.py`, `signals/http.py`, `provisioning.py`, `policy/store.py`, `signals/mapping.py`). | SG3, SG14; `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg3_credentials_are_refused_both_ways`, `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_a_producer_token_reaches_nothing_but_the_signal_intake`, `tests/test_signal_registration.py::test_an_unknown_group_and_a_non_admin_actor_are_refused`, `tests/test_signal_bundle_validation.py::test_clean_needs_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `tests/test_signal_mapping.py::test_clean_needs_every_record_from_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `tests/test_signal_evaluation.py::test_a_forged_clean_over_a_source_without_liveness_is_refused`, `tests/test_signal_replay.py::test_a_candidate_reading_clean_from_a_source_without_liveness_cannot_be_read` | Verified |
| T13.3 | S | The agent launders a verdict by choosing what the producer is shown | The input path is recorded at registration, and a `required` mapping admits only an `independent` source: validation and activation refuse an `agent_forwarded` one at its location, and resolution re-checks the source row it read (`policy/store.py`, `signals/resolve.py`). The input path is the owner's statement about how the producer is fed, which ExecBound cannot observe. | SG13; `tests/test_signal_bundle_validation.py::test_an_agent_forwarded_source_is_refused_in_a_required_mapping`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_an_agent_forwarded_source_is_refused_in_a_required_mapping` | Partial, see R31 |
| T13.4 | T | An envelope widens its own authority through tenant, capability, subject or severity | The envelope carries no tenant, space or principal, and a request naming one in an `x-tenant-id`, `x-principal-id` or `x-role` header or a query string is refused before it is read; the runtime role cannot write either table, so `signal_admit` is the only writer, and inside the fence it checks the capability, the subject kind and the producer's name and version against the registration, and the subject's account and provider against the token's own tenant's connector accounts, recording each refusal against the source; severity is stored, but resolution never reads it and no plan, audit event or view carries it, so the severity a producer claims changes no decision (`signals/http.py`, `signals/envelope.py`, `migrations/versions/0068_security_signals.py`). | SG1, SG6, SG7; `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_a_field_outside_the_registration_is_refused_and_recorded`, `::test_another_tenants_account_is_an_invalid_subject`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_a_request_shaped_outside_the_bounds_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_schema.py::test_the_runtime_role_cannot_write_either_table`, `tests/test_signal_views.py::test_severity_attributes_and_evidence_never_leave_the_record` | Verified |
| T13.5 | T | A conflicting duplicate or a reordered envelope flips a verdict | The first write wins per source and event id: a retry answers its original receipt, and different content under the same id is refused `CONFLICTING_DUPLICATE` and counted against the source; resolution reads each source's newest observation, so an older observation that arrives later never replaces it, and only a tie on observation falls to the later receipt and then to the event id in byte order (`migrations/versions/0068_security_signals.py`, `execution/snapshot.py`). | SG8; `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg8_a_conflicting_duplicate_keeps_the_first`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_a_retry_answers_its_receipt_and_a_conflict_keeps_the_first`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_a_later_observation_supersedes_an_earlier_one_whatever_the_arrival_order`, `::test_the_later_receipt_wins_a_tie_on_observation`, `::test_a_tie_is_broken_by_event_id_in_byte_order` | Verified |
| T13.6 | R | A producer disputes having sent a signal | Every record keeps the digest of its canonical form, its receipt time on the intake's clock and the registration version it was admitted under, and each plan that used it freezes those with the record, in the decision capture that replay reads; creating, rotating and revoking a source are audited under the acting ADMIN (`signals/envelope.py`, `resolution/models.py`, `provisioning.py`). | SG17; `tests/test_signal_envelope.py::test_the_canonical_form_and_its_digest_are_pinned`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_an_accepted_record_is_stored_with_the_intake_clock`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_the_newest_record_is_mapped_and_frozen`, `tests/test_signal_replay.py::test_four_records_at_maximum_width_fit_the_decision_capture`, `tests/test_signal_registration.py::test_every_change_to_a_source_is_audited_in_its_space` | Verified, see R3 |
| T13.7 | I | Prompts or reasoning traces leak into audit | The envelope has no field for them and refuses unknown fields, and the intake refuses secret-shaped text without quoting it; attributes are at most sixteen bounded scalars that stay in `security_signals` and never enter a plan, its decision capture, an audit event or a view; an approver sees the mapped value, the source's registered label and the capability, on the console and on the classic approval page, and only an administrator reads the frozen records, which hold no attributes (`signals/envelope.py`, `signals/intake.py`, `resolution/models.py`, `surfaces/console_api/approvals.py`, `surfaces/console_api/executions.py`, `surfaces/human.py`). | SG5; `tests/test_signal_envelope.py::test_anything_else_is_refused`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_secret_shaped_text_is_refused_and_never_quoted`, `tests/test_signal_views.py::test_an_approver_sees_the_value_the_label_and_the_capability`, `::test_an_approver_reads_the_capability_but_not_the_records`, `::test_an_administrator_sees_the_record`, `::test_severity_attributes_and_evidence_never_leave_the_record`, `::test_the_classic_page_names_the_label_and_the_capability_and_nothing_of_the_record` | Verified |
| T13.8 | I | `evidence_ref` used to make ExecBound fetch a URL | It is opaque text of at most 256 visible characters, refused with any control or invisible character, and it stays in `security_signals`: no frozen record, audit event or view carries it, and nothing fetches or links it (`signals/envelope.py`, `resolution/models.py`). | SG5; `tests/test_signal_envelope.py::test_invisible_and_control_characters_are_refused_in_free_text`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_an_envelope_with_every_field_is_stored_exactly_as_sent`, `tests/test_signal_views.py::test_severity_attributes_and_evidence_never_leave_the_record` | Verified |
| T13.9 | D | Flooding the intake or its storage | An 8 KiB body bound counted in bytes, a 5-second body deadline, 8 concurrent intake slots with an immediate 429, 600 envelopes per source per minute, and a cap of 500,000 records per source, counted over the source's whole life because nothing prunes them; `signal_admit` counts the last two in the transaction that refuses, and authenticates before it takes the source's row lock, so a caller without the token never waits behind a genuine admission (`signals/http.py`, `migrations/versions/0068_security_signals.py`). | SG4; `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_an_envelope_of_exactly_the_bound_is_read_and_one_byte_more_is_not`, `::test_a_slow_body_is_refused_at_its_deadline`, `::test_an_endless_body_is_cut_off_at_the_bound`, `::test_the_ninth_concurrent_intake_is_refused_at_once`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_the_rate_limit_refuses_commits_and_resets`, `::test_the_retention_cap_refuses`, `tests/test_signal_schema.py::test_a_caller_without_the_token_never_waits_for_the_sources_lock` | Verified, see R8, R32 |
| T13.10 | D | A producer outage blocks decisions | Deliberate: a required fact fails closed, the operator chooses in the bundle where a producer is required, and no producer is on the synchronous path; the gateway never calls one. A producer whose clock runs ahead has its newest record refused at every decision until the gateway's clock passes the observation, up to the intake's 60-second allowance; one that runs behind loses that much freshness; both fail closed. | SG10; `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_no_signal_is_refused_audited_and_nothing_runs`, `::test_a_newest_record_observed_after_the_decision_refuses`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_a_record_observed_after_the_decision_refuses`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_time_bounds` | Accepted |
| T13.11 | E | A signal satisfies a fact for another resource, principal or tenant | Exact correlation on account, provider, resource type and provider id inside the tenant; a source's principal scope, up to 64 agents or one group, is a predicate of the reader, so a record applies only to a request from an agent inside it; forced row-level security on both tables; the tenant comes from the token, and another tenant's fence never finds the source (`execution/snapshot.py`, `signals/resolve.py`, `migrations/versions/0068_security_signals.py`). Run-subject capabilities are not built, so every record's subject is a resource. A record scoped to a request reference never applies, even to a request that names it, because a request's `reference` is the agent's own string: the reader reads only unscoped records and the intake refuses a request reference (`signals/intake.py`), so a request-scoped capability will need a binding the agent does not choose. | SG1, SG5, SG12; `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_every_way_the_fact_is_not_there_is_invalid_context`, `::test_a_request_scoped_record_never_applies_even_to_the_request_it_names`, `::test_a_request_scoped_record_alone_is_no_record`, `::test_the_correlation_is_exact`, `::test_a_record_for_another_resource_never_reaches_the_fact`, `::test_a_source_scoped_to_another_agent_does_not_apply`, `::test_a_group_scoped_source_applies_only_to_its_members`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_a_request_reference_is_refused_before_the_token_or_the_writer`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_another_tenants_fence_never_finds_the_source`, `tests/test_signal_schema.py::test_the_catalog_names_both_tables_and_the_one_writer`, `::test_a_tenant_never_reads_another_tenants_sources`, `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg0_the_reference_producer_is_accepted_and_lands_in_its_own_tenant_only` | Verified |
| T13.12 | E | A source in one space satisfies a fact for an agent in another | The reader's space predicate is `spaces.space_clause`'s, and resolution re-checks the space on the row it read; validation and activation refuse a source that the bundle's own space, an offered bundle or the activating space cannot use; negative tests, because spaces have no row-level security second line and `signal_sources` is not among the tables the space isolation test reads through the shared predicate; these are the tests that test names as `signal_sources`' case (`execution/snapshot.py`, `signals/resolve.py`, `policy/store.py`), beside the label reader's (`surfaces/human.py`, R25). | SG12; `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_a_source_of_another_space_does_not_apply_unless_offered`, `::test_the_statement_never_reads_a_source_of_another_space`, `tests/test_signal_bundle_validation.py::test_a_source_of_another_space_is_refused_unless_offered`, `::test_activation_in_another_space_needs_a_source_that_space_can_use`, `::test_an_offered_bundle_admits_only_offered_sources` | Verified, see R25 |
| T13.13 | E | An approval overrides a missing required signal | Missing is `INVALID_CONTEXT`, which denies before any execution or approval exists. A person's decision refuses a plan whose source was revoked or whose record went stale, and dispatch refuses one whose source was revoked, each as `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`; an approval never outlives the signal's validity (`execution/snapshot.py`, `execution/approvals.py`, `execution/admission.py`). | SG10, SG11; `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_no_signal_is_refused_audited_and_nothing_runs`, `::test_an_approval_cannot_rescue_a_revoked_source`, `::test_an_approved_request_cannot_run_on_a_revoked_source`, `::test_a_decision_after_the_signal_goes_stale_is_refused`, `::test_a_person_may_decide_while_the_signal_holds` | Verified |
| T13.14 | E | A signal after dispatch alters a settlement | The plan is frozen, the last re-attestation is inside the transaction that commits dispatch, and settlement never reads a signal: a record that changes the value and commits while dispatch is being committed refuses it, and a record after dispatch changes neither the execution, its answer to a retry, nor its impact and claim (`execution/admission.py`, `execution/snapshot.py`). | SG16; `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_a_record_after_dispatch_changes_nothing`, `::test_a_record_arriving_while_dispatch_is_committed_refuses` | Verified |
